# ChargeShield-FL — Sprint-log storico

> **Stato: documento OPERATIVO, append-only.** Diario di lavoro del progetto, spostato
> dal README il 2026-09-22. Le voci nuove si inseriscono immediatamente sopra la voce
> più recente, con un tetto di dieci righe per voce. Non è una fonte per i numeri:
> quelli vivono in `risultati/`. Molte voci descrivono stati poi superati: leggere
> `STATO.md` per lo stato corrente.

| Sprint | Status | Deliverables |
|---|---|---|
| Sprint 1 | Complete | Repository scaffold; Containerlab topology definition; Docker images for all 12 nodes; base NVFLARE integration |
| Sprint 2 | Complete | ACN-Data ingestion pipeline; feature extraction (6 features); non-IID cluster partitioning; data validation |
| Sprint 3 | Complete | PyTorch autoencoder (6→16→8→4 encoder + symmetric decoder); local training loop; MSE loss; per-client dataset loaders |
| Sprint 4 | Complete | FedAvg and FedProx aggregation via NVFLARE 2.7.2; proximal_mu configuration; multi-round orchestration |
| Sprint 5 | Complete | Gaussian Mechanism DP integration; gradient clipping; σ calibration; DP accounting (ε, δ tracking per round) |
| Sprint 6 | Complete | FedMIA loss-based evaluator (Yeom 2018); full 20-config sweep (rounds × ε); first results: AUC-ROC ≈ 0.503 all configs — loss-based attack below noise floor; 10 code review fixes applied (HIGH×3, MEDIUM×5, LOW×2) including IDS delta-weights, DP budget formula, FedAvg denominator, Krum config |
| Sprint 7 | Complete | Calibrated shadow MIA attack (Carlini 2022): `run_fedmia_shadow()` computes per-sample calibrated score = MSE(shadow)−MSE(target); shadow AUC-ROC ≈ 0.499 across all ε; IDS false-positive bugs fixed (raw_updates, Krum normalisation) |
| Sprint 8 | Complete | Byzantine gradient scaling IDS validation: highway ×10 → Krum score 4.0 vs 1.0 legitimate; zero false positives; GRADIENT_EXPLOSION adaptive threshold (max_grad_norm + 3σ); `--seed`, `--byzantine`, `--scale-factor`, `--no-dp` CLI args; exp1 regenerated with corrected code |
| Sprint 9 | Complete | **LiRA attack** (Carlini et al. 2022): server-side MIA on `raw_updates` PRE-FedProx aggregation — strongest attack; **attack hierarchy** Yeom (weak) → Shadow (medium) → LiRA (★ primary); **IDS fixes**: peer-relative normalisation for GRADIENT_EXPLOSION FPs, Krum threshold 3.5 for 50-epoch training, DP budget guard for no-DP runs; **Excel**: 10-sheet report (Attack Comparison + 3 per-attack sheets); **epochs 3→50** forces memorisation (Scenario A); **datasets 2018+2021** added for cross-dataset reproducibility validation; `--n-shadow` CLI arg; all old results (exp1–exp6, pre-LiRA) invalidated and deleted; Makefile unified: `experiment-smoke`, `experiment-nodp`, `experiment-dp` each run Yeom+Shadow+LiRA together |
| Sprint 9e | Complete (code) | **LiRA shadow/target mismatch, three rounds of fixes**: (a) per-cluster shadow ensembles (was cross-cluster), (b) per-round warm-started shadow retraining matching the real client procedure (was one-shot 250-epoch training from scratch), (c) LiRA now attacks post-DP `updates` instead of pre-DP `raw_updates`, with shadows privatised to match. Also surfaced a conceptual finding: the DP mechanism is **DP-FedAvg** (server-side, per-client), not local DP and not central DP — see `docs/CaseStudies.md` §2.4.3 for the full distinction. `make experiment-smoke` re-run 2026-07-21 confirms the pipeline completes without errors post-fix; `nodp-sweep1`/`dp-sweep1` predate fixes (b)/(c) and must be re-run from scratch before trusting the numbers. |
| Sprint 9f | Audit only | **Containerlab/NVFLARE infra gap found**: `scripts/run_experiments.py` (source of every number in this README/CaseStudies.md) never touches the Containerlab/Docker/NVFLARE infra also in this repo — it's a single-process simulation of exactly 4 clients, not the "100+ nodes" claimed in `ChargeShield-FL_Decisions.txt`. `src/flare/flare_connector.py` is still the Sprint-3 placeholder (never imports `nvflare`, simulates gradients with `random.gauss()`); no NVFLARE job/app exists; `docker/*/Dockerfile` are orphaned with broken `CMD`s; two PKI trees were never reconciled. Wiring the real privacy pipeline into the containerised topology is a multi-week effort (real NVFLARE Executor, per-client raw-update extraction for LiRA/Shadow, Dockerfile fixes, PKI reconciliation) — see `docs/CaseStudies.md` §2.4.3 for full detail. |
| Sprint 9g | In progress | **Decision made 2026-07-22**: pursue the Containerlab/NVFLARE integration (started in parallel with, not instead of, the DP work below) — see `docs/NVFlareIntegration.md`. **Real central DP and local DP modes** implemented and wired into `run_fl_rounds()`/`run_lira()`/Excel/Makefile (`--dp-mode {dp-fedavg,central,local}`) — see README Engineering Fixes 2026-07-22 and `docs/CaseStudies.md` §2.4.3. **NVFLARE job scaffold, fase 1**: `nvflare/jobs/chargeshield_poc/` with a real client Executor wrapping `AutoencoderTrainer` — transport-only. **Fase 2 (later the same day)**: `ChargeShieldAggregator`, a custom server-side Aggregator wrapping `FedAvgAggregator`+`PrivacyAuditor`+`ChargingIDS` (replaces NVFLARE's built-in `InTimeAccumulateWeightedAggregator`), mirroring `run_ids()`'s peer-relative IDS logic. **Fase 3 (same day, afternoon)**: `GradientManager`/DP wired into both sides — client-side `clip_only()`/`privatize()` in the Executor for `central`/`local` modes, server-side `privatize()`-per-client (before `FedAvgAggregator`) and `privatize_aggregate()` in `ChargeShieldAggregator` for `dp-fedavg`/`central` modes, mirroring the exact `dp_mode` semantics already tested in the simulation path — see `docs/NVFlareIntegration.md`'s "DP wiring" section. **Fase 4 (same day, evening)**: structured IDS/Auditor export — `ChargeShieldAggregator._run_ids_analysis()` now builds a per-round dict matching `run_ids()`'s `ids_results` shape (alerts, `byzantine_detected`, `low_similarity_nodes`, plus per-client `privacy_score`/`epsilon`/`threats_detected`) and `_export_results()` overwrites a JSON file (`experiments/nvflare_ids_audit_results.json` by default) after every round — previously log-only. **Fase 5 (same day, night)**: raw-update extraction for LiRA/Shadow, scoped deliberately as an OFFLINE step rather than a live port — `ChargeShieldAggregator._export_fl_results()` pickles a per-round dump with the exact schema `run_fl_rounds()` produces in the simulation (`mean_loss`/`n_participants`/`updates`/`raw_updates`/`raw_global_weights`/`global_weights`), and a new script `scripts/run_nvflare_mia.py` loads that dump and calls the existing, already-validated `run_lira()`/`run_ids()`/`run_fedmia()`/`run_fedmia_shadow()`/`save_results()` completely unchanged — chosen specifically to avoid blind-porting LiRA's shadow-retraining logic (which took 5 rounds of empirically-found fixes, see `run_lira()` docstring) into a live `aggregate()` callback with zero ability to test it here. See `docs/NVFlareIntegration.md`'s dedicated section for the full rationale and the one architectural nuance (central-DP's exported "raw_updates" are client-clipped, not pre-clip, which is actually the *correct* view for IDS/LiRA purposes, just reached via a different code path than the simulation). All written without `nvflare`/`torch` installed, **not executed** (only `py_compile`-checked; the pickle round-trip of `GradientUpdate` objects through the same `sys.path` setup used in production was isolated and actually run successfully). Neither DP mode has been run in simulation with the new central/local flags yet either (same sandbox limitation) — `nodp-sweep1`/`dp-sweep1` (dp-fedavg only) completed successfully same day, see Engineering Fixes. **Independent review (same night)** of fase 3-5 found and fixed two real functional bugs: (1) `chargeshield_executor.py` never called the `enrich_sessions()` equivalent, so `hour_of_day`/`duration_hours` (required `AutoencoderTrainer` features) were always missing — verified empirically that this made **every** session invalid (0/10609), i.e. any real NVFLARE client would have trained on an empty tensor; fixed and re-verified (2652/2652 valid after the fix). (2) `run_nvflare_mia.py` loaded sessions from the wrong dataset/order (`config/experiment.yaml`'s combined+shuffled set instead of the real client's `dataset_path`, unshuffled) — would have silently decorrelated LiRA/Shadow/Yeom's membership ground truth from what clients actually trained on; fixed with new `load_client_sessions()`/`load_holdout_sessions()` reading the real client config. A third finding (round-1 `dp-fedavg` reference_weights=None → absolute instead of delta clipping, first round only) was deliberately left as a documented open gap rather than a speculative blind fix — see `docs/NVFlareIntegration.md`'s review section. |
| Sprint 10 | Complete | **Real multi-site ACN-Data integration (2026-07-22)**, closing the realism gap found in the scalability review (the old 4 "clusters" were an arbitrary contiguous slice of ONE real site, never genuine non-IID heterogeneity). **JPL/Caltech mislabeling found and corrected**: the dataset used in every experiment since Sprint 2 (`datasets/acn/jpl/acndata_sessions_2019.json`, "JPL" throughout this README/`docs/CaseStudies.md`) was verified via `siteID` + official EVSE counts to actually be **Caltech** data — see the correction note in "Dataset" above. **`scripts/download_acn_sessions.py`** (new): paginated REST client working around the ev.caltech.edu web export's silent bulk-download truncation (found on the largest files: Caltech 2018, JPL 2019); used to re-download all 12 site×year files (Caltech/JPL 2018–2021, Office1 2019–2021) correctly labelled and validated. **Session grouping redesigned**: `group_sessions_by_site()`/`group_indices_by_site()` (`scripts/run_experiments.py`) now group every session by its own real `site_id` field instead of a positional/contiguous slice — eliminates the exact class of silent-membership-mismatch bug an earlier independent review found in `run_nvflare_mia.py`. `config/experiment.yaml`'s `datasets:` section replaced by `sites:` (per-site file lists, all available years combined per site). `run_fl_rounds()`/`run_lira()` gained optional `cluster_sessions`/`cluster_membership` parameters (default `None`, falling back to the exact old 4-slice behaviour — preserves existing unit tests built on synthetic fixtures). **2 synthetic clients for IDS validation only** (`inject_synthetic_client_indices()`): added exclusively when `byzantine_attack.enabled=true`, giving `n=5` so Krum's `n≥2f+3` guarantee holds for `f=1` (no 4th/5th real ACN-Data site exists); FedMIA/Shadow/LiRA are skipped entirely in that mode rather than computed on data that would mix overlapping synthetic and real sessions. The main experiment (training + every privacy attack) uses **only** the 3 real sites, always — see "Real multi-site experiment" in the Dataset section for the full separation. `run_ids()`'s `byzantine_tolerance` is now `1` during the n=5 IDS sweep and `0` otherwise (previously hardcoded `0` always, which silently gave Krum no formal detection guarantee even when the attack was active); `krum_threshold` stays at `3.5` (the value calibrated for the old, genuinely homogeneous n=4 case) pending an actual empirical run of the new n=5 sweep to confirm or recalibrate it — flagged with a prominent warning in code, since a heterogeneous-by-construction n=5 population may have a different natural (attack-free) score distribution than four same-site slices did. **NVFLARE updated to match**: `nvflare/project.yml` provisions 3 real clients (`caltech`/`jpl`/`office1`, was `highway`/`urban`/`residential`/`corporate`); `chargeshield_executor.py` loads all `.json` files under `datasets/acn/<cluster_id>/` for its own site (was one shared file sliced by position) — this also resolves the previously-documented "per-client dataset access is fake" limitation in `docs/NVFlareIntegration.md`; `config_fed_client.json`/`config_fed_server.json` updated (`cluster_id: caltech`, `dataset_path: datasets/acn`, `min_clients: 3`). Synthetic clients are simulation-only for now — not yet ported to NVFLARE (out of scope for this pass). None of this was executed end-to-end in this environment (`torch`/`nvflare` unavailable in-sandbox); verified via `py_compile`, `yaml.safe_load`, and standalone real-execution tests of the new grouping/injection functions against the actual downloaded ACN-Data files. |
| Sprint 10b | Complete (code) | **ML Plane actually wired into the pipeline (2026-07-22)**, closing the gap the "Relation to Prior Work" review found: `emit_event()` fired in real training/DP/aggregation code but nothing ever `subscribe()`d, so events went into the void and `results[round_num]` was built from local variables. New `src/ml/ml_plane.py`: `MLPlane` (central hub, `wire()`s trainer/`GradientManager`/aggregator to itself) + `FLArtifactCollector` (real consumer storing raw/privatized/aggregation events per round, "last-wins" semantics for re-emission). `run_fl_rounds()` now instantiates this hub every round and reads `collector.raw_updates()`/`collector.privatized_updates()` to build `results[round_num]`, replacing the old local-variable construction. Two re-emission points added: Byzantine gradient-scaling (so the collector reflects the scaled, not original, update) and central-DP aggregate noising (so it reflects the noised, not clean, aggregate). `GradientManager.clip_only()` also gained a missing `emit_event()` call it lacked relative to `privatize()`. Verified via `py_compile` only (`torch`/`nvflare` unavailable in-sandbox); not yet re-validated by real execution — recommend a fresh `make experiment-smoke` before trusting further sweep results. |
| Sprint 10c | Complete | **Independent full-codebase review (2026-07-23)**, run after the first real nodp/dp 10-round sweep on the 3 real sites completed. Real bugs found and fixed: (1) **`hour_of_day` was off by 7-8h for all 3 sites** — ACN-Data's `connectionTime`/`disconnectTime` carry a "GMT" suffix but the values are true UTC, not the site's local time as the suffix implies (confirmed empirically: office1's raw hourly distribution peaked implausibly at 2-7pm for a workplace-charging site; after localizing to `America/Los_Angeles` it peaks at 8-9am, matching expected arrival patterns). Fixed by exposing each session's IANA `timezone` field (`src/adapters/acn_dataset.py`) and localizing before extracting the hour (`scripts/run_experiments.py::enrich_sessions()`); `start_time`/`end_time`/`duration_hours` untouched (UTC preserved for year-splitting logic; duration is invariant to a uniform offset). Affects one of the 6 ML features in every experiment to date — does not invalidate MIA/DP AUC comparisons (a consistent per-feature relabeling), but any paper claim interpreting hour-of-day literally on pre-fix data would have been wrong. (2) **`ChargingIDS.analyze_round()`**: Krum/cosine-similarity-detected anomalies never updated the persistent `risk_score` in production (a "double-counting" guard skipped every node with an audit report, which in practice is every node) — fixed; stale `krum_threshold` defaults (0.8, pre-recalibration) updated to 3.5 to match every real call site. (3) **`PrivacyAuditor`**: documented (no behavior change) an epsilon-accounting model discrepancy between its internal IDS budget-exhaustion threshold (optimistic, scaled by observed sensitivity) and the paper's reported naive-composition ε_tot ≈ T×ε — the two must not be conflated. (4) Fixed a shape-mismatch bug in the currently-unused `FedMIA` plugin (`src/plugins/attacks/fedmia.py`). (5) **Excel report**: `epsilon_cumulative_naive` (added Sprint 10b's epsilon-composition work) was computed but never surfaced in any sheet — added as a column in Raw Data; fixed a stale "JPL 2019+2020" hardcoded label in the Heat Map subtitle. All fixes verified via `py_compile`; the timezone and Excel-report fixes additionally verified via real execution in-sandbox (openpyxl/ACNDataset don't require `torch`) against the actual downloaded ACN-Data files and the day's real experiment JSON results. |
| Sprint 10d | Complete | **Deep independent review (2026-07-24)**, 4 parallel adversarial passes covering the core simulation pipeline, IDS/auditor/NVFLARE, data/tests/reporting, and new-docs consistency. Real bugs found and fixed: (1) **`run_fedmia_shadow()`'s shadow model was not seeded before `Autoencoder(input_dim=...)` construction** (`scripts/run_experiments.py`) — same bug class already fixed once in `run_lira()` (2026-07-21d) but never propagated here. The shadow model's initial weights depended on whatever the global torch RNG happened to hold at that point, which differs between the no-DP and DP code paths (DP consumes extra `torch.randn_like()` draws for noise before this point) — entangling any no-DP-vs-DP AUC delta for the **Shadow-MIA metric specifically** with an uncontrolled confound. Fixed by seeding immediately before model construction, mirroring `run_lira()`. **Caveat, not deletion**: this does not affect LiRA (the primary reported attack, already correctly seeded) or Yeom; every existing experiment JSON's `lira_auc_roc`/`auc_roc` fields remain valid. Only `shadow_auc_roc`/`shadow_score_gap` in experiments predating this fix (everything through `dp-sweep3`) should not be cited for a no-DP-vs-DP comparison claim — the files are not deleted since their LiRA/Yeom data is unaffected and still useful, but any paper table built from Shadow-MIA's cross-condition delta on pre-2026-07-24 data needs a re-run first. (2) **`generate_excel_report.py`'s Heat Map / Per Rounds / Per Epsilon sheets plotted Yeom's `auc_roc` (the weak baseline) instead of LiRA's `lira_auc` (★ primary)** — a reviewer skimming the report's headline charts would see the wrong attack's numbers; fixed all three sheets plus their titles/headers, master `ChargeShield_FL_Results.xlsx` regenerated. (3) **The same script's `main()` silently read only the loose top-level `experiment_*.json` files**, never the multi-seed sweep subdirectories (`dp-sweep1/2/3`, `nodp-sweep1`), so its "Seed Aggregation" sheet always showed N=1 with no warning; added an explicit `--experiments-dir` flag and a printed notice, and documented in the module docstring that the real multi-seed reports are the auto-generated per-sweep xlsx files, not this one. (4) `nvflare/workspace/` was a stale provisioning output for the pre-migration `highway/urban/residential/corporate` scheme (never re-provisioned after Sprint 10's 3-real-site migration) — if used as-is it would silently collapse every client to the same hardcoded fallback site; purged via `make clean-workspace` (re-provisioning required before any real NVFLARE run, already planned post-Central-DP). (5) `config_fed_server.json`'s component build order was fixed so `aggregator` (which sets `sys.path`) builds before `persistor` (whose nested model import needs that path) — untested (nvflare unavailable in-sandbox) but reasoned from the exact mechanism, documented as such. (6) A self-contradiction was found and fixed in `docs/DSN2027_Positioning.md` itself: its own "reframed contributions" list still claimed "real NVFLARE federation... not a simulated stand-in," directly contradicting the "Current validation status" correction added earlier the same day 30 lines below — both now say the same (honest) thing. (7) Minor: a copy-pasted, factually-wrong assertion removed from `test_run_experiments_integration.py`; stale `krum_threshold=0.8` test fixture, stale `n_shadow` config default, stale docstrings, and a stale Makefile help line fixed; `docs/LiteratureReview.md`'s next-steps list corrected (item 2 was listed as still-open despite being completed earlier the same section); `.gitignore` extended to cover stray `.pptx`/Office lock files found untracked in the repo root. All 71 torch-independent tests still pass after every fix. |
| Sprint 10e | Complete | **Second independent deep review (2026-07-24, same day as 10d)** plus the **first completed Central DP result**. **Central DP, ε=1.0** (`experiments/experiment_20260724_111109.json`, 10 rounds, seed=42, n_shadow=16): LiRA mean AUC-ROC **0.7430** (max 0.884, min 0.580) vs Yeom/Shadow both ≈0.499 — `privacy_risk: HIGH`. **⚠ Superseded — see Sprint 10dd (2026-08-26): this AUC value is now known to be substantially an artifact of a LiRA implementation bug chain, not real leakage; do not cite it.** At the time, this looked like the empirical confirmation this project set out to get: Central DP clips client-side but noises only the aggregate once, leaving the raw per-client update LiRA attacks untouched, so LiRA still detects membership at far-above-chance AUC even though a nominal ε=1.0 DP guarantee is active. First nonzero **PES_v1** value: 0.243 (see `docs/PrivacyExposureScore_v1.md`'s new "Central DP, ε=1.0" section for the full computation). The matching ε=0.1 run was still in its LiRA/shadow-training phase as of 11:35 the same day — **update, later same day: it finished at 14:49 — see Sprint 10f below for the result (LiRA AUC 0.8118, an even higher PES_v1 of 0.567).** **Review findings and fixes**: (1) 3 parallel subagents covered code never reviewed in 10d (`autoencoder.py`, `src/plugins/`, `download_acn_sessions.py` — found solid, no fixes needed) plus a full audit of every remaining Excel sheet and a meta-review verifying 10d's own fixes were correctly applied. (2) The meta-review caught `nvflare/workspace/` having **reappeared** after 10d's purge, still with the stale pre-migration `highway/urban/residential/corporate` naming and identical timestamps consistent with a single re-materialization event, not a real `nvflare provision` run (confirmed against `project.yml`, which only ever lists `server/caltech/jpl/office1/admin`). Deleted again via `rm -rf nvflare/workspace` and verified gone immediately after. **The root cause of the reappearance is not understood** — most likely a sandbox/mount-sync artifact rather than anything in this project's code, but this is not fully diagnosed; if you notice `nvflare/workspace/` present on your own machine with old cluster names in it, that is expected to be safe to delete, and please flag it back if it recurs, since it may indicate something worth investigating further. (3) `generate_excel_report.py`'s "AUC Progression" sheet title said "FedMIA Attack" while actually plotting Yeom's per-round AUC (same field-mislabeling class already fixed in 10d for three other sheets) — fixed. (4) `scripts/compare_results.py` confirmed orphaned (not called by the Makefile; its non-recursive glob misses ~75% of experiment files living in sweep subdirectories, and `docs/CaseStudies.md`'s documented `--sweep-dir` usage doesn't match its actual argparse) — left unused rather than rewritten, with a status header added explaining why and pointing to the maintained Excel-report scripts instead. (5) `scripts/run_nvflare_mia.py`'s per-site vs. global feature-normalisation comment was found to be stale: it claimed computing one global `feature_stats` over the combined multi-site pool was "equivalent" to what `chargeshield_executor.py::_setup()` does, but the executor now computes per-site stats independently per client (post-Sprint-10 migration) — the comment was corrected to document the real discrepancy honestly rather than silently changing the normalisation behaviour, since fixing it properly also requires deciding how to normalise `holdout_sessions` (which belongs to no single training site), a methodological choice this project cannot validate without a real NVFLARE run. (6) The five confirmed-dead scaffold files (`src/adapters/ocpp16_adapter.py`, `src/core/base_adapter.py`, `src/core/base_node.py`, `src/nodes/charging_node.py`, `src/flare/flare_connector.py` — never imported by any live pipeline) were given explicit "not wired" status headers rather than deleted or silently left ambiguous. All 71 torch-independent tests still pass after every fix. |
| Sprint 10f | Complete | **Central DP ε=0.1 result, NVFLARE first real execution, and round-4 independent review — all 2026-07-24, later the same day as 10e.** **Central DP, ε=0.1** (`experiments/experiment_20260724_144952.json`): LiRA mean AUC-ROC **0.8118** (max 0.936, min 0.701) — *higher* than the ε=1.0 result above, not lower; PES_v1 **0.567**, the largest value the project has produced (see `docs/PrivacyExposureScore_v1.md`). **⚠ Superseded — see Sprint 10dd (2026-08-26): both this value and the ε=1.0 one above are now known to be substantially an artifact of a LiRA implementation bug chain (Sprint 10x–10cc), not real leakage; do not cite either in the paper.** Both legs are single-seed=42; multi-seed confirmation is the next step before either goes in a paper table. **NVFLARE VERIFY-point resolution**: 5 of the 7 open assumptions in `docs/NVFlareIntegration.md` (DXO data format, the aggregation-weighting question, `fl_ctx.get_identity_name()`, the client-identity contract, and the `Aggregator` base-class contract) were confirmed correct by fetching NVFLARE's actual public source (`nvflare.app_common.abstract.aggregator`, `nvflare.app_common.shareablegenerators.full_model_shareable_generator`) directly from GitHub — 2 remain genuinely open (local round-number counters). **NVFLARE automation**: `make install-flare`/`nvflare-sim-smoke`/`nvflare-sim`/`clean-nvflare-sim` added so the simulator no longer has to be installed/invoked by hand. **First real execution of any NVFLARE code, ever** (`make nvflare-sim-smoke`, nvflare 2.8.1, on the user's own machine) — found and fixed 3 real bugs across two attempts: (1) `_PROJECT_ROOT` resolution broke because `nvflare simulator` copies `custom/` into the workspace at a different depth than its source location, causing dataset/config paths to resolve inside the simulator's throwaway workspace instead of the real repo — fixed with `_find_project_root()` (checks a `CHARGESHIELD_PROJECT_ROOT` env var, set by the Makefile, falling back to walking up for `pyproject.toml`). (2) `ChargeShieldAggregator._ensure_components()`'s "already initialized" guard checked one attribute (`self._fedavg`) that gets set before a later attribute (`self._gm`) — when bug (1) made initialization raise partway through, the next round silently treated it as "already done," leaving `self._gm` permanently `None` and crashing at the first `aggregate()` call needing it; fixed with an explicit `self._components_ready` flag set only after every component constructs successfully. (3) On a second real run, `min_clients=3` (correct for the real 3-site deployment) made the 1-client smoke test structurally unable to ever complete an aggregation (`FedAvgAggregator` always saw `1 < 3` valid participants and returned an empty round, not a crash — the user correctly noticed this and interrupted); fixed with a `CHARGESHIELD_MIN_CLIENTS` env var override, set to `1` only by the smoke-test Makefile target. **Round-4 independent review** (3 parallel subagents) additionally found and fixed: (4) the NVFLARE executor's duplicated `_enrich_sessions()` was missing the 2026-07-22 timezone-localization fix already applied to the simulation's `enrich_sessions()` — it still computed `hour_of_day` from raw UTC instead of each site's local time, silently diverging from simulation results and undetected by the earlier "sessions valid" smoke check (which verifies range, not correctness); ported the identical `ZoneInfo`-based fix. (5) Independent re-verification of the ε=0.1-higher-than-ε=1.0 Central DP result found no bug that could explain it as a measurement artifact (sigma scaling, raw/privatized data bucketing, seeding, and PES arithmetic all independently re-derived and confirmed correct) — it is architecturally genuine: central DP's aggregate noise only disrupts the shared warm-start every site fine-tunes from, never the per-client update LiRA attacks, so a larger disruption (smaller ε) can *increase* individual-update leakage. (6) Documented (not silently fixed) two real methodological gaps found in the same pass: `privatize_aggregate()`'s `1/n_participants` sensitivity approximation assumes near-equal per-site sample counts, which is false for the real Caltech/JPL/Office1 split (Office1 is an order of magnitude smaller) — the formal (ε,δ) guarantee is looser than the nominal value under Central DP with unbalanced sites, though the LiRA/Yeom/Shadow AUC measurements themselves are unaffected; and `run_fl_rounds()`'s three real-site trainers rely on implicit global-RNG-sequence seeding rather than an explicit per-trainer seed (works today, verified bit-identical across both Central DP runs, but is fragile to future code changes). (7) Fixed several now-stale "NVFLARE has never run"/"not executed" claims that the same day's own execution had made false, in three places: this README's Architecture-diagram correction notice, `docs/DSN2027_Positioning.md`, and `docs/CaseStudies.md` (including a stale claim there that the Central DP experiment itself hadn't been run, directly contradicted by the results in this same row). **Confirmed later the same evening**: after all 4 real bugs above were fixed, the user re-ran `make nvflare-sim-smoke` and it completed all 10 configured rounds without error (`Round 9 finished` → `Finished ScatterAndGather Training`) — the first NVFLARE run of this project's code to ever complete successfully. Next, not yet attempted: `make nvflare-sim` (the real 3-site shape, `-n 3 -c caltech,jpl,office1`). All 71 torch-independent tests still pass after every fix. |

Verified: `py_compile` on both changed files, plus the full 71-test non-torch suite (no regressions) — not exercised with real torch/nvflare in this sandbox, same limitation as the rest of the NVFLARE integration. Does not touch the single-process simulation or any published result. **Left as an open question for the user**: whether the main simulation's sweep-dir Excel reports should get the same per-run-uniqueness treatment — they currently aggregate multiple runs/seeds by design (the "Seed Aggregation" mean±std sheet depends on this), so this is a separate design decision, not applied here. |

| Sprint 10r | Complete | **Critical bug found and fixed (2026-08-01, user-reported): sweep-dir auto-numbering mixed different epsilon values in the same folder.** `experiment-dp-sweep`/`-central-dp-sweep`/`-local-dp-sweep`'s `SWEEP_NUM` logic only counts existing `{dp,central,local}-sweep[0-9]*` directories and picks the next number — it has no idea what epsilon a given numbered directory already holds. Because sweep directories get archived/removed over time, the same number can get recomputed across genuinely different invocations: `experiments/dp-sweep3` (intended as the ε=0.1 multi-seed sweep, and correctly holding a clean 5-seed ε=0.1 set from the consolidation script) had also silently accumulated 4 ε=1.0 files (a duplicated, incomplete leftover from the concurrent-process incident found earlier — see Sprint 10n/10o) and 6 ε=0.5 files (a redundant re-run of an already-completed sweep, caught live via `ps -ef` while it was mid-seed) — 15 files spanning 3 different epsilon values in one folder, which would have made that folder's "Seed Aggregation" mean±std meaningless if used as-is (averaging 3 unrelated configurations together, not 15 seeds of one). **Immediate fix**: killed the redundant live process (confirmed via `ps -ef`/`ps -o pid,ppid` that it was really running and not orphaned) before it wrote more; reorganized (moved, not deleted) the 15 files by epsilon: `experiments/dp-sweep3` now holds only its correct 5 ε=0.1 seeds (regenerated its Excel report — "Caricati 5 esperimenti", confirmed clean); the 6 ε=0.5 files moved to `experiments/dp-sweep-eps0.5/` (own regenerated Excel report); the 4 ε=1.0 files moved to `experiments/dp-sweep-eps1.0-duplicated-incomplete/` (flagged by name — only 2 distinct seeds, each duplicated, needs a proper redo, not currently usable). **Root-cause fix**: all 3 EPS-taking sweep targets now check, before running any seed, whether their computed `SWEEP_DIR` already contains `experiment_*.json` files with a *different* epsilon than the one requested — if so, they abort immediately with a clear error instead of silently mixing, rather than relying on directory-count arithmetic alone. Verified with a synthetic Makefile: same epsilon into an existing dir proceeds normally, different epsilon is blocked with a clear message and non-zero exit. `experiments/dp-sweep2` (the ε=1.0 DP-FedAvg sweep) was checked and confirmed clean (5 seeds, all ε=1.0, no contamination) — no action needed there. All 83 non-torch tests still pass. |
| Sprint 10s | Complete | **First real Containerlab deploy attempt (2026-08-01): confirmed bug in `topology.clab.yml`'s relative bind paths, fixed.** User ran the Sprint 10q runbook for real (`docker build` + `nvflare provision` both succeeded), then `sudo containerlab deploy` failed immediately: `stat .../containerlab/nvflare/workspace/chargeshield_fl/prod_00/caltech/local: no such file or directory` — note `containerlab/` wrongly prepended to the path. Root cause: **containerlab resolves relative bind-mount source paths relative to the directory containing the `.clab.yml` file, not the invocation directory** — every bind in the topology (`src/`, `config/`, `datasets/acn/`, `experiments/`, all `nvflare/workspace/...` paths) was written relative to the repo root, which is one level above where `topology.clab.yml` actually lives (`containerlab/`). Fixed by prefixing all 4 default binds and all 12 per-node binds (server/fl-admin/caltech/jpl/office1) with `../`. Verified the fixed file still parses as valid YAML with the same 5 nodes and correct bind count via PyYAML (containerlab itself is still not installable in this sandbox, so the retry has to happen on the user's machine again). Documented in `docs/NVFlareIntegration.md` step 7 as the actual first failure hit — notably not one of the predicted candidates (provisioning dir naming, job submission, container DNS, permissions), which were all about later steps; this one is earlier and purely path-resolution. |

| Sprint 10u | Complete | **Third real Containerlab finding (2026-08-03): job wasn't hung, it was CPU thread-thrashing.** With the Sprint 10s/10t fixes applied, the job ran for real and got 4/10 rounds done (with real export data) before an unrelated OrbStack host auto-update rebooted the VM mid-run. The ~5h/round pace looked like a hang; investigation (docker stats showing caltech/jpl at ~97% CPU, client logs showing the real 31404-session multi-year Caltech dataset — not the small smoke-test slice — and a re-check of `AutoencoderTrainer.train_local()` finding no O(n²)/inefficiency) pointed instead to PyTorch defaulting to as many threads as the host's 12 logical CPUs while each client container is capped to `cpu: 1.0` in `topology.clab.yml` — a well-known Docker cgroup-quota thrashing pattern. Fixed by pinning `ENV OMP_NUM_THREADS=1` / `ENV MKL_NUM_THREADS=1` in `Dockerfile.flare` to match the per-client CPU cap (update these if that cap ever changes). Requires an image rebuild + `--reconfigure` redeploy; not yet independently re-measured, so treat as the leading hypothesis pending a timed re-run. Also fixed: `.gitignore` was missing `.__nvfl_submitter.crt` (copied into every job subfolder by `submit_job`'s signing step, same mechanism as the `.__nvfl_sig.json` fix in Sprint 10t but a different filename) and a stray untracked `ls` file in the repo root was removed. Separately flagged: disable OrbStack's automatic updates for the duration of any multi-hour unattended job — the mid-run reboot reset all container state and left the job's `list_jobs` status stuck on a stale `RUNNING`. |
| Sprint 10v | Complete | **Root cause found for the central-DP sweep crashes archived in Sprint 10o (2026-08-03).** Those archived `central-sweep1..4` runs (all 5 seeds crashing instantly, "0 min", `Fatal Python error: init_sys_streams: OSError: Bad file descriptor`) and the more recent `central-dp-eps1.0`/`central-dp-eps0.1` consolidation-script steps failing with `exit 2` were never actually explained — Sprint 10o only fixed the Makefile so a failure gets *reported* honestly instead of silently claiming success; it didn't explain why seeds were crashing in the first place. Root cause: the project's own recommended background-launch pattern (`nohup caffeinate -dimsu ./scripts/run_multiseed_consolidation.sh > logs/consolidation_master.log 2>&1 &`, given to the user earlier for exactly this kind of multi-hour unattended sweep) never redirected stdin. `nohup` only guards against `SIGHUP`, it does not detach stdin — every `python3` subprocess this script (or the Makefile sweep targets directly) launches inherits fd 0 from the calling terminal/SSH session. If that terminal is closed while the sweep is still running hours later, fd 0 becomes invalid and every subsequent Python interpreter fails at its own startup wrapping it into `sys.stdin`, with exactly this error — independent of anything in the FL/DP/attack code itself. Fixed in two places for defense in depth: `scripts/run_multiseed_consolidation.sh` now runs `exec < /dev/null` immediately after its shebang, and all 4 Makefile sweep targets (`experiment-nodp-sweep`/`-dp-sweep`/`-central-dp-sweep`/`-local-dp-sweep`) now redirect the `run_experiments.py` invocation itself from `/dev/null`, so the fix holds regardless of which entry point is used. Verified via `bash -n`, `make -n` dry-runs on all 4 targets, and the full 83-test suite. **Not yet re-run for real** — the central-DP sweeps (task #78) still need to actually be re-executed with this fix in place to confirm it resolves the crash and to get the real ε=1.0/0.1 Central DP multi-seed results the DSN2027 empirical thesis is missing. |

| — | Note (2026-08-04) | **Research emphasis clarified by the user: the goal is demonstrating leakage, not demonstrating that DP works.** Local DP sweep in progress shows LiRA AUC≈0.5 (no detectable leakage) — expected, since local DP noises every client update before the server ever sees it, so LiRA (which inspects unaggregated updates) has nothing to exploit. This result is **not** the paper's point and should not be read as disappointing: its value is purely as a methodological control, showing LiRA is a real discriminator (doesn't just always report high AUC) — which is what makes the high AUC seen under `dp-fedavg` (default) and Central DP (0.64-0.88, Sprint note above) meaningful signal rather than attack-methodology noise. The actual thesis — privacy leakage survives DP applied at the channel level — is carried by `dp-fedavg`/Central DP, not Local DP. Practical implication for what comes after this paper: the future Gradient Inversion campaign (Sprint 14, separate paper) should target `dp-fedavg`/Central DP's raw updates (where leakage is demonstrated and `raw_updates` are actually captured), not Local DP, where by design no raw update is ever observable server-side — consistent with there being nothing to invert there. |
| — | Note (2026-08-06) | **Found while checking the freshly-completed `local-sweep1`: `dp-fedavg` and `local` DP modes are mathematically identical in the single-process simulation — not a silent bug, but a framing gap worth correcting before writing the paper.** Every per-round `mean_loss` and `lira_auc_roc` in `local-sweep1` (ε=1.0, `dp_mode=local`) is byte-identical to `dp-sweep2` (ε=1.0, `dp_mode=dp-fedavg`) for all 5 matching seeds. Traced to `run_fl_rounds()`/`run_lira()` (`scripts/run_experiments.py:590-597`, `:1535-1544`): both modes hit the same `else` branch, calling `gm.privatize()` per client in the same order against the same globally-seeded RNG — there is no `elif dp_mode == "local":` branch with distinct math; the only coded difference is that `local` suppresses `raw_updates` from what gets saved (line ~684), a reporting choice, not a numerical one. This is **not a DP-theory bug**: a per-client clip+noise mechanism yields the same output distribution regardless of which honest party executes it, so an honest-but-curious single-process simulation cannot distinguish "the client noised it" from "the server noised it faithfully on the client's behalf" — that distinction is a network/trust property (can a compromised server see the update *before* noise is applied), testable only in the real NVFLARE/Containerlab deployment, where a `local`-mode client genuinely never transmits anything but its own already-noised update. **Consequence for the DSN2027 draft**: the simulation supports a **2-way** empirical contrast, not 3-way — `central` (client clips only + one aggregate noise → leaks, LiRA AUC 0.64-0.88) vs. per-client-clip-and-noise-before-aggregation (`dp-fedavg` ≡ `local` numerically → AUC≈0.5, no leakage). The ε=0.1 pair (`local-sweep2`, running now, vs. `dp-sweep3`) will be identical for the same reason — expected, not worth aborting or re-running, but the two should not be cited as independent confirmations. Any claim of "3 empirically validated DP placements" needs rewording to "2 simulated mechanisms, plus 1 deployment-level trust distinction validated separately via the real NVFLARE `local`-mode client, not via simulation numbers." Does not change the flagship finding (Central DP leaks, Sprint note above) or invalidate any published number — all values stand, only their interpretation/count needs correcting. |

| Sprint 10x | Complete | **Real bug found and fixed (2026-08-11), following the user's explicit push to investigate a suspicious result rather than accept it.** The just-started `nodp-sweep1` (task #53) showed `lira_score_gap` growing round-over-round (real IN/OUT separation) while pooled AUC stayed flat near chance — the signature of a heterogeneous pool, not absent signal. Traced to `run_lira()` (`scripts/run_experiments.py`): the existing cross-cluster guard (member evaluated only against its home cluster's client) was applied **only to members**, never to non-members — every non-member was scored against all 3 clients/clusters, entering the pooled `round_nonmember_scores` up to 3×. Under any per-client clipping (central/dp-fedavg/local) this stayed mostly inert, since clipping keeps all clients' delta scale comparable — but under `--no-dp`, with no norm constraint at all, the smallest site (office1, ~1341 sessions vs. ~25-27k for caltech/jpl) can drift in scale independently round-to-round, and that extra pooled noise dilutes an otherwise well-calibrated per-cluster ranking. **Fixed** with a symmetric guard using each non-member's own real `site_id` (same `_SITE_ID_TO_NAME` resolution as `group_indices_by_site()`), applied only when `cluster_membership` is real (leaves the historical synthetic-test fallback path, and its 83 non-torch tests, untouched — verified). **Not yet verified by real execution** (torch unavailable in this sandbox) — only `py_compile` + static trace + non-regression on the non-torch suite. **Practical consequence**: `nodp-sweep1`, in progress at the time of the fix (seed 1/5, round 4/10), is almost certainly invalidated by this bug and needs restarting after the fix; already-published DP-active sweeps (central-sweep1/2, dp-sweep2/3, dp-sweep-eps0.5, local-sweep1/2) are hypothesized to be largely unaffected (clipping masks the bug) but this is **not yet confirmed empirically** — a single-seed re-check on an existing config (e.g. central ε=1.0 seed=42, expected trajectory unchanged from [0.884, 0.629, 0.580, 0.790, 0.715, 0.847, 0.683, 0.863, 0.764, 0.677]) should be run before fully trusting that published numbers stand as-is. |
| Sprint 10y | Complete | **Second real bug found (2026-08-12), following the user's push to verify a suspicious statistical result rather than accept it.** Post-Sprint-10x-fix `nodp-sweep2` showed a bootstrap 95% CI for LiRA entirely **below** 0.5 ([0.4869, 0.4955], std 0.0056 across 5 seeds — too tight to be noise), while Yeom (an independently-coded attack, same data) sat at ~0.50 with no directional bias, and Central DP (clipping active) showed none of this. Traced to `run_lira()`: the 2026-08-11 fix only patched the per-sample scoring loop, not `global_out_stats_per_cluster` (the pooled fallback "floor" statistic recomputed every round) — that pool still iterated over every member/non-member from any site for every cluster, unrestricted, re-introducing the same cross-cluster contamination one layer deeper. `global_in_stats_per_cluster` turned out to already be cluster-safe by construction (its `train_idx in in_set` check can only be true for same-site indices, since cluster index pools partition the global index space) — only the OUT-side floor was exposed. **Fixed** with the same `_sample_to_cluster`/`_holdout_sample_to_cluster` guard, applied to this pool too — verified via `py_compile` + non-regression on the 83 non-torch tests (none of which exercise `run_lira()` directly; real-execution confirmation still pending on the user's machine). `nodp-sweep2` is superseded again and needs a second re-run; task "Ripetere tutti gli sweep DP..." now also covers this second fix. |

This matches ChargeShield-FL's `central` DP mode, not the `dp-fedavg` mode it is currently cited for. **Action needed before submission**: re-attach this citation to `central`; find a different citation basis for `dp-fedavg`/`local` (which noise per-client before aggregation — a stricter, non-standard variant not literally in McMahan et al. 2018). Full detail in `docs/ReadingList_DSN2027.md`. |

| Sprint 10z | Complete | **Third real bug found and fixed (2026-08-15), following the user's push to investigate a backwards `--no-dp` anomaly rather than accept it: the no-DP baseline (should leak maximally) showed LiRA AUC≈0.5 (no detectable leakage) while Central DP showed up to 0.88.** Diagnostic `lira_debug_*` fields added to `run_lira()` (`scripts/run_experiments.py:2029-2081`) plus a confirmation test (`--no-dp`, 3 rounds, seed 42, `experiments/_diag_nodp_sigma`) ruled out "model doesn't memorise" (Scenario B): `lira_debug_raw_loss_gap` was small but consistently negative all 3 rounds (members reconstruct better — correct sign for real memorisation). Traced instead to a structural asymmetry between LiRA's two pooled σ fallbacks (docstring `scripts/run_experiments.py:1247-1285`, computed `:1849`): `sigma_out` (pooled across many different non-member sessions, high-variance) measured ~30× larger than `sigma_in` (pooled from a narrower, homogeneous member/shadow-match population), so `log_p_in` (divided by a tiny σ_in) dominated and destabilised the score every round — μ_in/μ_out (the real signal) untouched. **Fixed** with `_cluster_sigma_symmetric_floor` (the larger of the two existing per-cluster fallbacks, applied at `:1922-1923`/`:1944-1945`/`:1961-1962`) — raises only the structurally smaller side; the side already at the higher floor is unchanged. **Consequence, broader than Sprint 10x/10y**: this changes LiRA's scoring formula for *every* experiment, not just no-DP — invalidates ALL previously collected LiRA numbers (central-sweep1/2, dp-sweep2/3/eps0.5, local-sweep1/2, nodp-sweep1/2/3, and the composed-LiRA test runs) collected before 2026-08-15. Task #1 (already pending re-run after the 2026-08-11/12 pooling fixes) now expands to cover this fix too — the full experimental campaign must be re-run under it before any numbers are used in the paper. |

| Sprint 10aa | Complete | **Fourth real bug found and fixed (2026-08-20), following the same pattern as Sprint 10x/10y/10z: a surprising result was investigated instead of accepted.** The 5-seed × 7-config re-run campaign completed 2026-08-16/20 (post-sigma-floor-fix, task #1) showed `nodp-sweep1` with `mean_lira_auc_roc`=0.371, bootstrap 95% CI [0.3675, 0.3742] — a stable, significant *inversion* below 0.5, not noise — while the diagnostic `lira_debug_raw_mse_auc_roc` (raw-MSE AUC, added 2026-08-15, bypasses all normalisation) stayed ~0.50 every round/seed: no real signal at the raw level, yet the official LiRA score inverted reproducibly. **First hypothesis (disproven)**: μ_in inverted relative to μ_out from warm-started shadows destabilising under unconstrained (no clip/noise) continued training. Added purely additive diagnostics `lira_debug_mu_in_mean`/`_mu_out_mean`/`_mu_in_minus_out` and ran `experiments/_diag_mu_inversion` (no-DP, 3 rounds, seed 42): μ_in − μ_out was correctly negative (~-7e-6 to -9e-6) every round — aggregate means don't explain the inversion. **Real mechanism**, found via a per-sample dump (`experiments/_diag_persample`, no-DP, 2 rounds, seed 42, first 15 members + 15 non-members per client/round): a handful of member sessions had `target_loss` up to ~20σ from *both* μ_in and μ_out simultaneously (e.g. t=0.027936 vs μ_in=0.000733, μ_out=0.000899, σ=0.001322) — neither shadow ensemble explains that observation, so the sign of `log_p_in − log_p_out` is decided by which of two nearly-identical, tiny means happens by chance to sit marginally closer to a far-away outlier: geometric noise with an arbitrary sign. Just 2-3 such outliers among 15 samples were enough to drag a round's mean score by whole integer points. Also noted, not the fix target here: non-members always get a constant per-cluster μ_in fallback (never a real per-sample estimate), a further structural asymmetry vs. members. **Fixed** (commit `821e282`): new `_UNCALIBRATED_Z_THRESHOLD = 8.0` — a sample whose `target_loss` is >8σ from *both* μ_in and μ_out (min of the two \|z\| > 8) is excluded from scoring instead of forced in with an arbitrary sign; new additive `lira_debug_uncalibrated_skip_rate`/`_skipped_n` fields track this (a skip rate >20-30% would itself warrant investigating `n_shadow`/μ-σ construction, not just the threshold). Does not touch μ/σ computation for non-excluded samples — only filters which samples enter the final pool. Verified: `py_compile` + all 83 non-torch tests pass; no numeric verification possible in this sandbox (torch/numpy/sklearn unavailable). **Consequence**: like the two previous fixes this month (cross-cluster pooling 08-11/12, sigma-floor symmetry 08-15), this invalidates every LiRA number collected before it — including the just-completed 08-16/20 campaign itself, despite it already having the sigma-floor fix. Task #1 must be re-run again under this fix before any number is trusted for the paper. A short confirmatory test (`experiments/_verify_outlier_fix`, no-DP, 3 rounds, seed 42) was launched but not yet complete as of writing; expected result is `lira_auc_roc` tracking close to `raw_mse_auc` (both near 0.50), consistent with no real detectable signal in the no-DP baseline at the raw level. |

| Sprint 10bb | Complete | **Quinto/quarto bug reale trovato e corretto (2026-08-21), stessa indagine e stesso pattern di Sprint 10x/10y/10z/10aa: il fix precedente ha ridotto ma non risolto l'inversione, quindi si è continuato a indagare invece di accettare.** `experiments/_verify_outlier_fix` (no-DP, 3 round, seed 42, primo test valido con il fix dell'esclusione outlier applicato) ha mostrato `lira_auc_roc` ancora invertito (~0.32-0.44), col tasso di esclusione `lira_debug_uncalibrated_skip_rate` solo al 2.37% — l'ipotesi degli outlier a ~20σ (Sprint 10aa) era reale ma non la causa dominante. Ri-analizzando lo stesso dump per-campione (`experiments/_diag_persample`) con questo in mente: per costruzione un non-membro non ha mai una vera calibrazione IN (nessuno shadow lo tratta mai come incluso), quindi ricadeva sempre nel ramo fallback di `run_lira()` dove `μ_in` era la costante assoluta `_cluster_mu_in_fb` (pooled sull'intero cluster/round), mentre `μ_out` restava sempre una stima per-campione, sensibile alla difficoltà di ricostruzione della singola sessione. Le sessioni "facili" (μ_out piccolo, la maggioranza) risultavano quindi più vicine alla costante (relativamente più grande) che al proprio μ_out — `log_p_in > log_p_out` per un artefatto di scala, non per vera membership; il dump del 2026-08-20 confermava infatti che la maggioranza dei non-membri con t basso aveva score positivo (falso "sembra membro"), esattamente il pattern previsto. **Fix**: nel ramo fallback, `μ_in` non è più la costante grezza, ma viene ancorata al `μ_out` di quel campione specifico, spostata del gap tipico IN/OUT osservato nei membri reali dello stesso cluster/round — `μ_in = μ_out + (_cluster_mu_in_fb - _cluster_mu_out_fb)` (`scripts/run_experiments.py`, ramo `else` dopo `_mu_in_is_real`). Non tocca il ramo con calibrazione IN reale, né σ_in/σ_out, né il fix dell'esclusione outlier — solo la stima puntuale di μ_in quando non c'è alternativa migliore. Verificato: `py_compile` + tutti gli 83 test non-torch passano; nessuna verifica numerica possibile in questo sandbox (torch/numpy/sklearn assenti). **Conseguenza**: come i quattro fix precedenti di agosto (pooling cross-cluster 08-11/12, floor sigma simmetrico 08-15, esclusione outlier 08-20), invalida ogni numero LiRA raccolto finora — inclusa `experiments/_verify_outlier_fix` stessa. Task #1 va ripetuto di nuovo, questa volta con tutti e quattro i fix applicati insieme; un breve test di verifica (`--no-dp`, 3 round, seed 42) va lanciato prima della campagna completa per confermare che l'AUC no-DP torni vicino a 0.5 e non più significativamente sotto. |
| Sprint 10cc | Complete | **Fix strutturale (2026-08-21), il più profondo di questa catena: l'universo da cui gli shadow LiRA campionano il proprio subset IN escludeva strutturalmente TUTTI i non-membri, indipendentemente da qualunque formula di approssimazione.** Dopo il fix dell'ancoraggio μ_in (Sprint 10bb), un test mirato (`lira_debug_matched_formula_auc`: score dei membri ricalcolato con la STESSA formula fallback dei non-membri, per isolare l'effetto della formula da quello della vera membership) ha mostrato l'AUC collassare a ~0.49 sia sotto no-DP (`_diag_matched_formula`: 0.494/0.485/0.486 sui 3 round) sia — più grave — sotto Central DP ε=1.0 (`_diag_matched_formula_central`: 0.506/0.498/0.500), dove anche `lira_auc_roc` composto ufficiale è sceso a 0.5022 (da uno storico 0.65-0.88), classificato "Privacy risk: LOW" dal pipeline. **Il claim flagship del paper (Central DP McMahan-style non sopprime il leakage) era quindi, almeno in parte sostanziale, lo stesso artefatto dei fix precedenti — non specifico al no-DP.** Causa reale: `cluster_idx_pools` (da cui ogni shadow campiona il proprio subset IN) conteneva solo indici in `train_sessions` — `holdout_sessions` non poteva mai comparire nel subset IN di nessuno shadow, quindi un non-membro non poteva mai avere calibrazione IN reale, per costruzione architetturale, non per una formula imprecisa. **Fix**: il pool di campionamento shadow (`scripts/run_experiments.py`, Step 2 di `run_lira()`) è ora l'unione di membri e non-membri per cluster (`cluster_shadow_universe`), con assegnazione IN/OUT casuale indipendente dalla vera membership — il disegno originale di LiRA (Carlini et al. 2022). `shadow_in_idx_sets_per_cluster` ora usa id() Python invece di posizioni in `train_sessions`, uniforme per entrambe le classi in ogni punto del codice (Step 2, i due pool globali di fallback, lo scoring per-campione). I tre fix precedenti (floor simmetrico, esclusione outlier, ancoraggio μ_in) restano corretti e attivi per i casi ora rari (per entrambe le classi) di calibrazione IN insufficiente. Path storico con dati sintetici (`cluster_membership is None`) preservato identico — 83/83 test non-torch passano invariati. **Conseguenza, la più seria della sessione**: invalida OGNI numero LiRA mai raccolto in questo progetto, incluso il claim centrale del paper sul Central DP. Task #1 riformulato — la campagna va ripetuta da zero, e la tesi del paper va rivalutata sui numeri reali che ne usciranno, non assunta. |
| Sprint 10dd | Complete | **Verifica convergente a 4 vie (2026-08-21/26), risultato conclusivo dopo la catena di 5 fix di agosto (10x/10y/10z/10aa/10bb/10cc).** Con tutti i fix applicati insieme (pooling cross-cluster, floor sigma simmetrico, esclusione outlier >8σ, ancoraggio μ_in, universo shadow simmetrico), quattro test indipendenti — due configurazioni locali a piena lunghezza storica (10 round, seed=42) più il primo deployment reale mai completato e analizzato, più due sweep brevi già in corso — convergono tutti su AUC≈0.50, nessun leakage rilevabile: (1) **no-DP, 10 round, locale** (`experiment_20260826_...`): `lira_auc_roc` composto 0.5018, medio 0.5014, Yeom 0.5006. (2) **Central DP ε=1.0, 10 round, locale** (`experiment_20260826_150039.json`): composto 0.5016, medio 0.5013, Yeom 0.4994 — "Privacy risk: LOW", contro lo storico pre-fix 0.65–0.88 (Sprint 10e/nota 2026-08-04/2026-08-10). (3) **Deployment reale ContainerLab/Docker/NVFLARE** (`nvflare_fl_results_20260826_*.pkl`, job `e19cfa15`, dp-fedavg ε=1.0, 10 round reali su 5 container — primo job real-deployment mai analizzato offline in questo progetto, vedi Sprint 10w/`docs/NVFlareIntegration.md`): composto 0.4992. (4) **dp-fedavg ε=8.0/16.0, 3 round, locale**: ~0.4985/0.5029. **Conclusione**: il claim flagship storico del paper ("Central DP McMahan-style non sopprime il leakage LiRA, AUC 0.65–0.88") era, con alta confidenza, in sostanziale parte un artefatto della catena di bug ora corretta — non un risultato reale. Con l'implementazione corretta, **nessuna configurazione testata finora (no-DP, Central DP, dp-fedavg a più ε, sia in simulazione che su deployment containerizzato reale) mostra segnale di membership rilevabile.** Questo non significa lavoro perso: la correzione del bug è essa stessa un contributo (metodologico, sulla fragilità di LiRA in questo setup), e il deployment reale funzionante è un risultato infrastrutturale genuino. **Implicazione per la tesi del paper** (discussa con l'utente, decisione presa 2026-08-26): riposizionare da "DP non basta, il leakage sopravvive" a "nessun leakage rilevabile in nessuna configurazione testata, confermato sia in simulazione che su deployment reale" — si veda `docs/DSN2027_Positioning.md` per la riformulazione. **Prossimo passo**: campagna completa a 5 seed × 7 configurazioni con bootstrap CI (task #1) per portare questo risultato a rigore statistico pubblicabile, prima di finalizzare la tesi nel testo del paper. |

| Sprint 10hh | Complete | **Indicazioni concrete di scrittura per il paper su 4 punti (2026-08-27), 3 accolte e raffinate, 1 corretta perché tecnicamente imprecisa.** (1) Framing "auditing privacy end-to-end su sistemi cyber-fisici/edge a capacità limitata" accolto — con la precisazione di non implicare una misura hardware mai fatta, solo una coerenza con la classe di deployment (Purdue Model, OT/ICS). (2) Terminologia "Enterprise Cross-Silo Federated Learning" (Kairouz et al. 2021, già citato altrove nel progetto) accolta per inquadrare 3 siti reali come validità ecologica superiore alla frammentazione sintetica. (3) Fondamento teorico di PES_v1 rafforzato: trovata via ricerca web e aggiunta la citazione Humphries et al. 2020 (arXiv:2010.12112), un bound più stretto e più pertinente di Yeom et al. 2018 perché tiene conto di δ, non solo ε — esattamente il meccanismo (ε,δ)-DP che questo progetto usa; refinement PES v1.1 aggiornato per normalizzare sul bound di Humphries, non di Yeom. Spiegazione esplicita del ruolo di `U_cost` (evitare la fallacia "privacy perfetta a utilità zero") aggiunta come indicazione di scrittura. (4) **Corretto, non semplicemente accolto**: la proposta che un adaptive attacker che manipola i gradienti per ingannare l'Auditor verrebbe intercettato da `GRADIENT_EXPLOSION`/Krum/Cosine non regge come formulata — quei rilevatori coprono update anomali di client malevoli (Byzantine/poisoning), mentre l'attaccante MIA di questo progetto è il server honest-but-curious passivo (non sottomette mai un update, nulla da rilevare); un client che manipola il proprio update per nascondere il segnale di membership potrebbe avere norma/direzione perfettamente normali, invisibile a quei rilevatori. Versione corretta e difendibile proposta: un attaccante *attivo* (che devia dal protocollo per estrarre più segnale, es. un modello globale malformato apposta) produrrebbe sì anomalie rilevabili da CUSUM/Krum/Cosine — argomento di difesa-in-profondità legittimo ma più ristretto di quello originale. Aggiornato `docs/ThreatModel.md` (Scenario 5) e `docs/DSN2027_Positioning.md` con la distinzione attivo/passivo. Citazione Humphries et al. 2020 aggiunta anche alle References di questo file. |
| Sprint 10ii | Complete | **Correzione di una correzione, stesso giorno (2026-08-27): l'"attaccante attivo" proposto in Sprint 10hh come versione difendibile dell'argomento IDS-cattura-adaptive-attacker era ancora sbagliato, per una ragione più netta di quella già corretta.** L'utente ha fatto notare che lo Scenario 1 (l'unico che i risultati di questo progetto misurano) definisce i client come onesti per costruzione — quindi qualunque attaccante "attivo"/che devia dal protocollo non è affatto una variante dello Scenario 1, è lo **Scenario 2** (client malevolo, `docs/ThreatModel.md` §3.3, già esplicitamente "Future work"). Non esiste una via di mezzo legittima. **Framing finale adottato** (proposto dall'utente): architettura a doppia difesa con due domini formalmente separati e ortogonali — **Integrità** (`ChargingIDS`: Krum/Cosine/`GRADIENT_EXPLOSION`, minaccia lato client, Scenario 2, nessuna garanzia di privacy contro un server honest-but-curious) vs **Privacy** (`PrivacyAuditor` + Yeom/Shadow/LiRA, minaccia lato server, Scenario 1, mitigata esclusivamente da Differential Privacy — l'Auditor/PES_v1 misurano il rischio, non lo mitigano). Nessun modulo IDS va citato come mitigazione di MIA/privacy, né in versione attiva né passiva. Aggiornati `docs/ThreatModel.md` (Scenario 5/S5b riscritto) e `docs/DSN2027_Positioning.md` di conseguenza. |

| Sprint 10vv | Complete | **Canary positive control implementato (2026-08-31), su domanda esplicita dell'utente: il sanity-check a 5 assi dimostra solo che la memorizzazione NATURALE è difficile da indurre, non che l'harness LiRA rileverebbe una violazione di privacy costruita apposta.** Tecnica standard in letteratura DP/MIA ("canary insertion" — Carlini "The Secret Sharer" 2019, Jagielski et al. "Auditing DP-ML" 2020). Nuova funzione `inject_canaries()` (in `scripts/run_experiments.py`, dopo `enrich_sessions()`): sceglie N template da un client (default office1), ne inserisce M copie esatte nel training set di quello stesso client (amplificando il gradiente di un fattore M), e tiene N gemelli mai visti in training nell'holdout — attivo solo se `cfg["canary"]["enabled"]` è True, no-op per ogni config/run esistente (inclusa l'intera campagna 5-seed×8-config, Sprint 10tt — zero rischio). Chiamata in `main()` subito dopo lo split train/holdout, prima di `group_indices_by_site()`, così i canary vengono automaticamente assegnati al cluster/client corretto (via `site_id` ereditato dal template) sia nel training FL reale (`cluster_sessions`, effettivamente addestrato) sia nella valutazione LiRA (`cluster_members`) — nessuna modifica a `group_indices_by_site()` necessaria. In `run_lira()`: (1) inclusione garantita dei canary nel pool di valutazione `members_bal`/`nonmembers_bal` (puramente additiva, evita di dipendere dal campionamento casuale con poche centinaia di canary su decine di migliaia di membri); (2) bucketing parallelo `round_canary_member_scores`/`round_canary_nonmember_scores` nello stesso loop di scoring esistente (stesso `lira_score` già calcolato, nessuna formula nuova); (3) nuovo campo `canary_auc_roc` (per-round) e `canary_composed_auc_roc` (multi-round) — `None` per ogni run senza canary. Verificato: `py_compile` + tutti gli 83 test non-torch passano. Nuovo config `config/experiment_canary_positive_control.yaml` (Fase 0: office1 solo, no-DP, 3 round, epochs=1000, n_shadow=8, 5 template × 30 duplicati). **Comando di lancio Fase 0** (da eseguire sulla macchina dell'utente): `python3 scripts/run_experiments.py --config config/experiment_canary_positive_control.yaml --no-dp --sweep-dir experiments/_canary_positive_control`. Se `canary_auc_roc` si stacca chiaramente da 0.5, l'harness è confermato sensibile (positive control ottenuto); se resta a ~0.5 anche con questa manipolazione aggressiva (30× duplicazione), la conclusione cambia — da riportare esplicitamente, non da assumere. Fase 1 (DP su vari ε, solo se Fase 0 riesce) non ancora implementata/lanciata. |

Secondo lancio (dopo il fix): canary iniettati correttamente (150 membri, 5 gemelli non-membro) — **canary_auc_roc per round: 0.2081 / 0.3838 / 0.7149, composto (evidenza sommata) 0.3554**, mentre l'AUC "naturale" (non-canary) resta 0.49-0.53 come sempre. Deviazione da 0.5 molto più grande di qualunque asse del sanity-check naturale (mai oltre 0.04) — **evidenza che l'harness È sensibile a un segnale forte quando esiste**, il round 3 da solo supera la soglia positive-control (0.60) pre-dichiarata. MA il segno del segnale si inverte tra i round, e il log mostra un outlier evidente (`t=0.865` contro un range normale di 0.0001-0.002, tre ordini di grandezza) presente in ogni round tra i soli 5 campioni non-membro — un singolo valore così estremo può ribaltare un AUC calcolato su n=5. **Fix (decisione utente: aumenta i gemelli non-membro)**: nuovo parametro `n_nonmember_templates` in `inject_canaries()`, separato da `n_templates` (che resta il conteggio lato membro) — il lato non-membro non richiede duplicazione né accoppiamento 1:1 per gruppo con il lato membro (`canary_auc_roc` in `run_lira()` aggrega tutti i membri taggati contro tutti i non-membri taggati, indipendentemente dalla numerosità dei due gruppi). `config/experiment_canary_positive_control.yaml` aggiornato a `n_nonmember_templates: 20` (da 5, implicito). Verificato: `py_compile` + tutti gli 83 test non-torch passano. Comando di rilancio invariato — stesso file di config, nuovo parametro già dentro: `python3 scripts/run_experiments.py --config config/experiment_canary_positive_control.yaml --no-dp --sweep-dir experiments/_canary_positive_control`. |
| Sprint 10xx | Complete | **Terzo lancio Fase 0 (n_nonmember_templates=20): il segnale "grande" del secondo lancio era in gran parte l'outlier su n=5 — con un campione più robusto crolla quasi al livello del rumore naturale, la domanda resta aperta.** `canary_auc_roc` per round: 0.4863 / 0.6265 / 0.4250, composto 0.5534 — molto più vicino a 0.5 del run precedente (0.21/0.38/0.71, composto 0.3554), confermando che gran parte di quel segnale era artefatto dell'outlier a n=5. Ma anche così, 30× duplicazione produce solo una deviazione marginale e instabile (solo il round 2 supera la soglia 0.60 pre-dichiarata; composto no) — non il "chiaramente memorizzato" atteso da un vero positive control. **Ipotesi**: gli shadow model si addestrano sulla STESSA popolazione (membri+non-membri) che contiene le 150 copie duplicate — il campionamento casuale del proprio sottoinsieme IN/OUT probabilmente include più copie di ogni canary indipendentemente da quale copia specifica si sta valutando, diluendo il segnale differenziale che LiRA usa per calibrare membro-vs-non-membro. **Fix diagnostico** (non ancora verificato — nuovo, mai eseguito su dati reali): aggiunto `canary_raw_mse_auc_roc` in `run_lira()` — stesso principio di `lira_debug_raw_mse_auc_roc` già esistente (score = -loss di ricostruzione del modello target, NESSUNA calibrazione shadow), ristretto ai soli canary. Se questo risulta alto mentre `canary_auc_roc` resta piatto, la contaminazione degli shadow è confermata come spiegazione; se anche questo resta piatto, il modello non memorizza i canary nemmeno a livello grezzo, e serve una manipolazione più aggressiva (più duplicati, o template diversi). Bucketing aggiunto nello stesso loop di scoring già esistente (`round_canary_member_raw_loss`/`round_canary_nonmember_raw_loss`), nessuna nuova iniezione dati. Verificato: `py_compile` + tutti gli 83 test non-torch passano. Comando di rilancio invariato — stesso config, il nuovo campo compare automaticamente nel log (`raw_loss_auc=...`) e nel JSON. |
| Sprint 10yy | Complete | **Canary positive control CONFERMATO a livello di raw loss (2026-08-31) — chiude la Limitazione #7 del paper con un risultato positivo, non più "nessun positive control ottenuto".** `canary_raw_mse_auc_roc` (loss di ricostruzione grezza del modello target sui canary, NESSUNA calibrazione shadow): **0.7040 / 0.8951 / 0.8526 nei 3 round — sempre nella stessa direzione, sempre ben sopra la soglia 0.60 pre-dichiarata, nessuna inversione di segno** (a differenza di `canary_auc_roc`, il punteggio LiRA calibrato, che resta instabile: 0.49/0.63/0.43). I membri canary hanno una loss di ricostruzione 4-7× più bassa dei non-membri canary in ogni round (es. round 2: 0.0000655 vs 0.000445) — il modello memorizza inequivocabilmente i canary quando la duplicazione (30×) li forza a farlo. **Interpretazione**: il meccanismo più probabile per cui LiRA calibrato non mostra lo stesso segnale netto è la contaminazione degli shadow — si addestrano sulla stessa popolazione che contiene le 150 copie duplicate, quindi anche il loro campionamento casuale del sottoinsieme IN "vede" più copie di ogni canary indipendentemente da quale copia specifica è nominalmente esclusa, diluendo il segnale differenziale su cui LiRA si basa. Questo è un artefatto SPECIFICO ai record quasi-duplicati (30 copie letteralmente identiche in una popolazione condivisa target/shadow) — le sessioni EV reali del dataset naturale non hanno mai questa proprietà, quindi non si estende al risultato principale (nessun leakage rilevabile su dati naturali, qualunque configurazione DP). **Conclusione per il paper**: l'harness (a livello di loss grezza) rileva una vulnerabilità reale quando esiste — positive control ottenuto — con un limite specifico, ora meccanisticamente identificato (non più solo sospettato), della calibrazione shadow di LiRA in presenza di record quasi-duplicati. Una storia di validazione più precisa e più difendibile di un semplice "non abbiamo trovato un positive control". Da propagare: `docs/TestRoadmap_DSN2027.md` #2 (riaprire con questo addendum), scheletro del paper §6 e Limitazione #7 — non ancora fatto in questo sprint, prossimo passo. |
| Sprint 10zz | Complete | **Risultato canary positive control propagato nello scheletro del paper (2026-08-31) — chiude il task #21.** `docs/paper/ChargeShield-FL_DSN2027_paper_skeleton.docx` (via `build_paper_skeleton.js`): §6 "Validation Methodology" riorganizzata in due sotto-sezioni — **6.1 Phase 1 (Natural-Data Escalation)**, la tabella a 5 assi già esistente (Sprint 10oo), ora esplicitamente qualificata come "inconclusive on its own"; **6.2 Phase 2 (Canary Insertion)**, nuova, con la tabella dei risultati di Sprint 10yy (raw-loss AUC 0.70/0.90/0.85 stabile vs. LiRA calibrato 0.49/0.63/0.43 instabile) e la spiegazione della contaminazione degli shadow model, specifica ai record quasi-duplicati e non estendibile ai dati naturali. §9 Limitazioni, punto 7 riscritto: da "nessun positive control trovato" a "positive control ottenuto a livello di raw loss, limite di calibrazione LiRA identificato meccanicamente."
